\displaystyle\leq f(\lambda)^{2}\int\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{J}_{\mu_{\lambda}}\left(k,l\right)\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{4}g_{1}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}.

(F.37)

The
\widehat{J}_{\mu_{\lambda}}\left(k,l\right)
term produces the required factor of
\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)
, so (after using symmetry in
k
) we only need to bound the expression

\int\left(2\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)\right)\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{4}g_{1}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}.

(F.38)

We partition our integral using open
\varepsilon
-balls around the poles where
\varepsilon^{2}\leq\tfrac{1-C_{1}}{C_{2}}
- the constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
coming from
(.2)
. Let
B_{\varepsilon}(p)
denote the open
\varepsilon
-ball around
p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
n\in\left\{0,1,2,3\right\}
, let

A_{n}:=\left\{l\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:l\in B_{\varepsilon}(p)\text{ for precisely $n$ elements }p\in\left\{k,0,-k\right\}\right\}.

(F.39)

Firstly it is possible for
A_{3},A_{2}=\emptyset
if
\left\lvert k\right\rvert
is sufficiently large compared to
\varepsilon
. It is also easy to see that
A_{3}\subset B_{\varepsilon}(0)
and
A_{2}\subset B_{2\varepsilon}(0)
. We will partition
\mathbb{R}^{d}
into
A_{3}
,
A_{2}
,
A_{1}
, and
A_{0}
and bound the
l
-integral on each part. Recall that
(.2)
implies that
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\leq C^{-1}_{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{-2}
for
\left\lvert l\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon
and
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\leq C^{-1}_{2}\varepsilon^{2}
for
\left\lvert l\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon
. Also recall the pointwise (
F.16
) and integral (
F.20
) bounds we derived for the
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors. We treat
A_{3}
first:

\displaystyle\int_{A_{3}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{4}g_{1}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\leq\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)\cap B_{\varepsilon}(k)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{4}\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(k)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\leq\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}=\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{d-6}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-6}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

(F.40)

\displaystyle\int_{A_{3}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{4}g_{1}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\int_{A_{3}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l+k\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(-k)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l+k\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(k)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}=\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{d-6}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-6}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

(F.41)

Recall
\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}=d\pi^{\frac{d}{2}}/\Gamma\left(\tfrac{d}{2}+1\right)
is the surface area of an unit
d
-sphere. In these calculations we used Hölder’s inequality and increased the domain of integration to get upper bounds. These integrals are finite for
d>6
, and approach
0
if we take bounded
\varepsilon
.

For
A_{2}
the counting of cases is the only extra complication. A precise counting is entirely possible, but unnecessary for our purposes. It is simple to see that the arguments outlined for
A_{3}
can be applied to get the bound as an integer multiple of

\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{4}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)=\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{d-6}+\frac{1}{d-4}+\frac{1}{d-2}\right)\varepsilon^{d-6}.

(F.42)

Essentially, these terms arise because given a pair of two distinct points from
\left\{k,0,-k\right\}
, there are terms with three, two, and one factor(s) of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
centred on these two points. This bound is finite for
d>6
, and approaches
0
if we take bounded
\varepsilon
.

Repeating this for
A_{1}
, we get the bound as an integer multiple of

\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{4}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)=\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{d-4}+\frac{1}{d-2}+\frac{1}{d}\right)\varepsilon^{d-6}.

(F.43)

Essentially, these terms arise because given a single point from
\left\{k,0,-k\right\}
, there are terms with two, one, and zero factor(s) of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
centred on this single point. This bound is finite for
d>4
, and approaches
0
if we take bounded
\varepsilon
.

The
A_{0}
integral is qualitatively different. By taking the integral bound for the
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors and a uniform (on
A_{0}
) bound for the factors of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
, we get the bound

\frac{C^{3}}{C^{3}_{2}}\varepsilon^{-6}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.44)

As in Lemma
F.3
, if the
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
term dominates, then we have a sufficient bound for our result. The largest of the other terms (for
d
sufficiently large and
\varepsilon\leq 1
) is the
\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{d-6}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-6}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}
term. Like we did in Lemma
F.3
, we can make these other terms smaller by decreasing
\varepsilon
at the cost of making the
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
bound greater. The largest term can be minimised (up to a constant) by taking
\varepsilon(d)=2\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{\frac{1}{d}}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2d}}\left(1-\frac{6}{d}\right)^{\frac{1}{d}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{d}}
for some fixed
A>0
. For this choice, we have both integrals of the order

\varepsilon^{-6}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}=2^{-6}\pi^{-3}A^{-\frac{6}{d}}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{d}}\left(1-\frac{6}{d}\right)^{-\frac{6}{d}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}+1\right)^{-\frac{6}{d}}=\left(\frac{\text{e}}{2\pi}\right)^{3}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{d}}\frac{1}{d^{3}}\left(1+o\left(1\right)\right),

(F.45)

as
d\to\infty
.

Bounding the
\left\lVert\varPhi^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,k}\varPhi\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}
term is similar. For each
\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we define the integral linear operator
\left(\varPhi^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,k}\varPhi\right)\left(\overline{x}\right)\colon L^{2}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)\to L^{2}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)
as the integral operator with kernel function

\left(a,b\right)\mapsto\left(\varPhi^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,k}\varPhi\right)\left(\overline{x};a,b\right)\\
:=\int\varphi^{(2)}\left(\overline{x}-\overline{u};a,c\right)\tau_{\lambda,k}\left(\overline{u}-\overline{u}^{\prime};c,c^{\prime\prime}\right)\varphi\left(\overline{u}^{\prime};c^{\prime},b\right)\mathrm{d}\overline{u}\mathrm{d}\overline{u}^{\prime}\mathcal{P}^{\otimes 2}\left(\mathrm{d}c,\mathrm{d}c^{\prime}\right).

(F.46)

We once again take
\mathcal{P}
-positive sets
E_{1},E_{2}
and corresponding
g_{1},g_{2}
. Then we use the Fourier inversion theorem and Cauchy-Schwarz to get

\left\langle g_{1},\left(\varPhi^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,k}\varPhi\right)\left(\overline{x}\right)g_{2}\right\rangle\leq f(\lambda)\int\widehat{J}_{\mu_{\lambda}}\left(k,l\right)\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{4}g_{1}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}.

(F.47)

Then once again the
\widehat{J}_{\mu_{\lambda}}\left(k,l\right)
term produces the required factor of
\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)
, so (after using symmetry in
k
) we only need to bound the expression

\int\left(2\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)\right)\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{4}g_{1}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}.

(F.48)

Counting the number of factors of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
centred on each of the poles
\left\{k,0,-k\right\}
then tells us that there is a uniform bound that is some integer multiple of

\frac{C^{2}}{C^{2}_{2}}\left(\int_{B_{\varepsilon}(0)}\left(\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{4}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}+\varepsilon^{-4}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\\
=\frac{C^{2}}{C^{2}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{d-4}+\frac{1}{d-2}+\frac{1}{d}\right)\varepsilon^{d-4}+\frac{C^{2}}{C^{2}_{2}}\varepsilon^{-4}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.49)

Once again, if the
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
term dominates the result is proven. Otherwise, we let
\varepsilon
vary with
d
. The largest term can be minimised (up to a constant) by taking
\varepsilon(d)=2\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{\frac{1}{d}}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2d}}\left(1-\frac{4}{d}\right)^{\frac{1}{d}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{d}}
for some fixed
A>0
. For this choice, we have both integrals of the order

\varepsilon^{-4}\beta^{2}=2^{-4}\pi^{-2}A^{-\frac{4}{d}}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{d}}\left(1-\frac{4}{d}\right)^{-\frac{4}{d}}\Gamma\left(\frac{d}{2}+1\right)^{-\frac{4}{d}}=\left(\frac{\text{e}}{2\pi}\right)^{2}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{d}}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(1+o\left(1\right)\right),

(F.50)

as
d\to\infty
. For large
d
, this is dominated by
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{d}}d^{-3}
and therefore
\left\lVert\varPhi^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,k}\varPhi\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}
is dominated by
\left\lVert\varPhi^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}\mathcal{T}_{\lambda,k}\varPhi\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}
and we prove the result.

∎

The following lemma allows us to deal with occurrences of
\overline{W_{k}}
.

See
5.20

Proof.

The first inequality follows by applying the cosine-splitting result (Lemma
5.9
) to (
F.2
).

For the
\tau_{\lambda}
term in
\overline{W_{k}}
we apply (
F.1
) and (
F.2
) to get

\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{x};a,b)\leq\varphi(\overline{x};a,b)+\lambda(\varphi\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b)+\lambda^{2}(\varphi\tau_{\lambda}\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b).

(F.51)

Similarly, with the additional use of (
5.55
), we get

\tau_{\lambda,k}(\overline{x};a,b)\leq\varphi_{k}(\overline{x};a,b)+2\lambda\left((\varphi_{k}\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b)+(\varphi\varphi_{k})(\overline{x};a,b)\right)+4\lambda^{2}(\varphi\tau_{\lambda}\varphi_{k})(\overline{x};a,b)\\
+4\lambda^{2}(\varphi\tau_{\lambda,k}\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b).

(F.52)

By using Lemma
F.2
, the Fourier inversion theorem, and
\lambda\leq f(\lambda)
, we can realise that we can bound
\overline{W_{k}}
by bounding the following objects. For
\vec{j}\in\left\{1,2,3\right\}\times\left\{1,2,3,4,5\right\}
, define

\mathcal{W}(k;\vec{j}):=\int P^{j_{1}}_{1,2}(l)Q^{j_{2}}_{3,4}(l;k)\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}},

(F.53)

where

\displaystyle P^{j}_{1,2}(l)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\left\lvert\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{2}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=1\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=2\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{2}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=3,\end{cases}

(F.54)

\displaystyle Q^{j}_{3,4}(l;k)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=1\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=2\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=3\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=4\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=5,\end{cases}

(F.55)

where
\left\{g_{m}\right\}_{m=1}^{4}
are the functions of the form
g_{m}(a)=\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}(E_{m})}\mathds{1}_{E_{m}}(a)
arising from the application of Lemma
F.2
.

For the
P^{j}_{1,2}(l)
factor we can get bounds as we did in the proof of Lemma
5.16
. For
Q^{j}_{3,4}(l;k)
we need to do a bit more processing. For
j=1
,

\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}(E_{3})\mathcal{P}(E_{4})}\int_{E_{3}\times E_{4}}\left\lvert\widehat{\varphi}_{k}(l;a,b)\right\rvert\mathcal{P}^{\otimes 2}(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}b)\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}\\
\leq C\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right),

(F.56)

where in the last inequality we used Lemma
5.18
. For
j=2
, we use Cauchy-Schwarz to get

\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)^{2}g_{3}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq C\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.57)

An identical argument gives the same bound for
j=3
. For
j=4
, we can use the self-adjointness of
\widehat{\varPhi}(l)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)^{2}\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{3}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq f(\lambda)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{3}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\lambda)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{3}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}},

(F.58)

and similarly

\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq f(\lambda)\widehat{J}_{\mu_{\lambda}}\left(k,l\right)\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{3}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq Cf(\lambda)\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l;k)\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{3}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}},

(F.59)

where we write for convenience

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3};k)=\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}-k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}+k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}-k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}+k).

(F.60)

Note that all the bounds for the
Q^{j}_{3,4}(l)
terms have a
\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)
factor. So we only need to prove that the remaining
l
-integral of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
and
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors is bounded.

As in our previous proofs, we partition
\mathbb{R}^{d}
into the sets
A_{n}
defined earlier in (
F.39
). For the
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
, and
A_{3}
parts we then use the bounds
C
and
C^{2}
for the various
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors and
1/\left(C_{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}\right)
to bound the
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
factors. By using the techniques used previously in this section, we can show that these contributions is bounded by some
\varepsilon
and
d
(only) dependent constant. Since we don’t need to show decay, we don’t need to worry about taking
\varepsilon
to
0
.

For the
A_{0}
contribution, we bound
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
factors by
1/\left(C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)
and show that the integral of the
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors over all of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is bounded. For
j_{2}\neq 1
this proceeds similarly to the argument in the proof of Lemma
5.16
. For
j_{2}=1
we need to take a step back. These versions of (
F.53
) arise from trying to bound one particular type of term, which we now treat separately. We bound

\esssup_{\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\mathcal{E}}\int\tau_{\lambda}\left(\overline{x}-\overline{u};a_{1},a_{2}\right)\varphi_{k}\left(\overline{u};a_{3},a_{4}\right)\mathrm{d}\overline{u}\leq\esssup_{a_{3},a_{4}\in\mathcal{E}}\int\varphi_{k}\left(\overline{u};a_{3},a_{4}\right)\mathrm{d}\overline{u}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(0)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}.

(F.61)

Then Lemma
5.18
gives the result.

∎

We are now only left with bounding
\overline{H_{k}}
.
See
5.21

Proof.

We begin by defining a sightly different object to
\overline{H_{k}}
. Given
\vec{a}=\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{16}\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{16}
, define

\displaystyle H^{\prime}\left(\overline{x}_{1},\overline{x}_{2},\overline{x}_{3};\vec{a};k\right):=

\displaystyle\int\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{s}-\overline{x}_{1};a_{1},a_{2})\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{u};a_{3},a_{4})\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{v}-\overline{s};a_{5},a_{6})\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{v}+\overline{x}_{2}-\overline{t};a_{7},a_{8})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{s}-\overline{w};a_{9},a_{10})\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{w}-\overline{u};a_{11},a_{12})\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{t}-\overline{w};a_{13},a_{14})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\tau_{\lambda,k}(\overline{t}+\overline{x}_{3}-\overline{u};a_{15},a_{16})\mathrm{d}\overline{t}\mathrm{d}\overline{w}\mathrm{d}\overline{z}\mathrm{d}\overline{u},

(F.62)

so we have

\overline{H_{k}}\leq f(\lambda)^{5}\esssup_{\overline{x}_{1},\overline{x}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\vec{a}\in\mathcal{E}^{16}}H^{\prime}\left(\overline{x}_{1},\overline{x}_{2},\overline{0};\vec{a};k\right).

(F.63)

We then proceed to bound
H^{\prime}\left(\overline{x}_{1},\overline{x}_{2},\overline{x}_{3};\vec{a};k\right)
to get our result. This form is preferred because it more easily indicates what the Fourier arguments should be when we take a Fourier transform. A schematic Fourier diagram for this can be found in
[
HHLM22
, Figure 2]
.

We then expand out the
\tau_{\lambda}
and
\tau_{\lambda,k}
terms. For each of the
\tau_{\lambda}
terms we apply (
F.1
) and (
F.2
) to get

\tau_{\lambda}(\overline{x};a,b)\leq\varphi(\overline{x};a,b)+\lambda(\varphi\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b)+\lambda^{2}(\varphi\tau_{\lambda}\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b).

(F.64)

Similarly, with the additional use of the cosine-splitting lemma, we get

\tau_{\lambda,k}(\overline{x};a,b)\leq\varphi_{k}(\overline{x};a,b)+2\lambda\left((\varphi_{k}\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b)+(\varphi\varphi_{k})(\overline{x};a,b)\right)+4\lambda^{2}(\varphi\tau_{\lambda}\varphi_{k})(\overline{x};a,b)\\
+4\lambda^{2}(\varphi\tau_{\lambda,k}\varphi)(\overline{x};a,b).

(F.65)

By using Lemma
F.2
, the Fourier inversion theorem, and
\lambda\leq f(\lambda)
, we can realise that we only need to get appropriate bounds for the following objects. For
\vec{j}\in\left\{1,2,3\right\}^{7}\times\left\{1,2,3,4,5\right\}
, define

\mathcal{H}(k;\vec{j}):=\int P^{j_{1}}_{1,2}(l_{1})P^{j_{2}}_{3,4}(l_{1})P^{j_{3}}_{5,6}(l_{2})P^{j_{4}}_{7,8}(l_{2})P^{j_{5}}_{9,10}(l_{1}-l_{2})P^{j_{6}}_{11,12}(l_{1}-l_{3})P^{j_{7}}_{13,14}(l_{2}-l_{3})\\
\times Q^{j_{8}}_{15,16}(l_{3};k)\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}},

(F.66)

where

\displaystyle P^{j}_{m,n}(l)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=1\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=2\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=3,\end{cases}\qquad\text{for }m,n\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,14\right\},

(F.67)

\displaystyle Q^{j}_{15,16}(l;k)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=1\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=2\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=3\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=4\\
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert&:j=5,\end{cases}

(F.68)

where
\left\{g_{m}\right\}_{m=1}^{16}
are the functions of the form
g_{m}(a)=\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}(E_{m})}\mathds{1}_{E_{m}}(a)
arising from the application of Lemma
F.2
. If we can bound these
\mathcal{H}(k;\vec{j})
appropriately, then we can combine them to get the required bound.

We now recall pointwise bounds for the
P^{j}_{m,n}(l)
and
Q^{j}_{15,16}(l)
terms. In the proof of Lemma
5.20
we derived

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}(0)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}

(F.69)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{m}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{n},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{n}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

(F.70)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq f(\lambda)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{m}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{n},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{n}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}},

(F.71)

and

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}\leq C\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)

(F.72)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\left\langle g_{16},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{16}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

(F.73)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{15}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

(F.74)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}_{k}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\lambda)\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{15}\right\rangle

(F.75)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda,k}(l)\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{16}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq Cf(\lambda)\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l;k)\left\langle g_{15},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{15}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle g_{16},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{16}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}

(F.76)

where

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l;k)=\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k).

(F.77)

Also recall that we can bound
\left\lvert\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{n}\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq C
and
\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{m}\right\rangle\leq C^{2}
if required. Note that all the bounds for the
Q^{j}_{15,16}(l)
terms have a
\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)
factor. We therefore only need to show that the remaining integrals of
\widehat{\varPhi}
and
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
terms can all be bounded by some constant multiple of
\beta^{2}
. Also note that the bounds for
Q^{1}_{15,16}(l)
,
Q^{2}_{15,16}(l)
, and
Q^{3}_{15,16}(l)
only differ by a uniform constant (after we bound
\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{m}\right\rangle\leq C^{2}
), and so once we have dealt with one of these we have dealt with the other two.

Let us first consider
\vec{j}=\left(1,1,1,1,1,1,1,1\right)
. After applying the uniform pointwise bound for
Q^{1}_{15,16}(l)
, we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to bound

\displaystyle\int\left\lvert\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{2}\right\rangle\right\rvert\left\lvert\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{4}\right\rangle\right\rvert\left\lvert\left\langle g_{5},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})g_{6}\right\rangle\right\rvert\left\lvert\left\langle g_{7},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})g_{8}\right\rangle\right\rvert\left\lvert\left\langle g_{9},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{2})g_{10}\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\times\left\lvert\left\langle g_{11},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})g_{12}\right\rangle\right\rvert\left\lvert\left\langle g_{13},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})g_{14}\right\rangle\right\rvert\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{2}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{4}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{5},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})g_{6}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{13},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})g_{14}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\left(\int\left\langle g_{7},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})g_{8}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{9},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{2})g_{10}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{11},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})g_{12}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.78)

To deal with these parentheses, we use a volume-preserving change of variables and factorise each into three integrals. For example,

\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{2}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{4}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{5},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})g_{6}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{13},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})g_{14}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\\
=\left(\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{2}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})g_{4}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\left(\int\left\langle g_{5},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})g_{6}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{2}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\left(\int\left\langle g_{13},\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{3}^{\prime})g_{14}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{3}^{\prime}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right).

(F.79)

There are now two types of integral we need to bound:

\displaystyle\int\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{n}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}\left(E_{m}\right)^{2}\mathcal{P}\left(E_{n}\right)^{2}}\int\left(\int_{E_{m}\times E_{n}\times E_{m}\times E_{n}}\widehat{\varphi}(l;a,b)\widehat{\varphi}(l;c,d)\mathcal{P}^{\otimes 4}\left(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}b,\mathrm{d}c,\mathrm{d}d\right)\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}\left(E_{m}\right)^{2}\mathcal{P}\left(E_{n}\right)^{2}}\int_{E_{m}\times E_{n}\times E_{m}\times E_{n}}\left(\varphi(\cdot;a,b)\star\varphi(\cdot;c,d)\right)\left(\overline{0}\right)\mathcal{P}^{\otimes 4}\left(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}b,\mathrm{d}c,\mathrm{d}d\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\esssup_{a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi(\cdot;a,b)\star\varphi(\cdot;c,d)\right)\left(\overline{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\esssup_{a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{E}}\int\varphi(\overline{x};a,b)\varphi(-\overline{x};c,d)\mathrm{d}\overline{x}\leq\esssup_{a,b\in\mathcal{E}}\int\varphi(\overline{x};a,b)\mathrm{d}\overline{x}\leq C,

(F.80)

\displaystyle\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{2}\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\esssup_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{8}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi(\cdot;a_{1},a_{2})\star\varphi(\cdot;a_{3},a_{4})\star\varphi(\cdot;a_{5},a_{6})\star\varphi(\cdot;a_{7},a_{8})\right)\left(\overline{0}\right)\leq g(d).

(F.81)

In (
F.80
) we used
\varphi\left(-\overline{x};c,d\right)\in\left[0,1\right]
and
(.1)
. In (
F.81
) we bounded the convolution of four adjacency functions using
(.3)
. The result of this is that we can bound (
F.78
) by
C^{\frac{5}{2}}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Since
g(d)\leq\beta^{4}
, this satisfies the bound we require. As noted above, this argument also proves the required bound for
\vec{j}=\left(1,1,1,1,1,1,1,2\right)
and
\vec{j}=\left(1,1,1,1,1,1,1,3\right)
.

Now suppose that we change some subset of
\left\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{7}\right\}
from having value
1
to having value
2
. The argument proceeds similarly, except that after we have factorised our integrals we now want to also bound integrals of the following types:

\displaystyle\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{1}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}\left(E_{1}\right)^{2}\mathcal{P}\left(E_{2}\right)^{2}}\int\left(\int_{E_{1}\times E_{1}\times E_{2}\times E_{2}}\widehat{\varphi}^{(2)}(l;a,b)\widehat{\varphi}^{(2)}(l;c,d)\mathcal{P}^{\otimes 4}(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}b,\mathrm{d}c,\mathrm{d}d)\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\frac{1}{\mathcal{P}\left(E_{1}\right)^{2}\mathcal{P}\left(E_{2}\right)^{2}}\int_{E_{1}\times E_{1}\times E_{2}\times E_{2}}\left(\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a,b)\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;c,d)\right)(\overline{0})\mathcal{P}^{\otimes 4}(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}b,\mathrm{d}c,\mathrm{d}d)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\esssup_{a,b,c,d\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a,b)\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;c,d)\right)\left(\overline{0}\right)\leq g(d),

(F.82)

\displaystyle\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{1}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)g_{4}\right\rangle^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\esssup_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{8}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{1},a_{2})\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{3},a_{4})\star\varphi(\cdot;a_{5},a_{6})\star\varphi(\cdot;a_{7},a_{8})\right)\left(\overline{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}(0)\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}^{2}\esssup_{\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{1},a_{2})\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{3},a_{4})\right)\left(\overline{x}\right)\leq C^{2}g(d)

(F.83)

\displaystyle\int\left\langle g_{1},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{1}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{2},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{2}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{3},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{3}\right\rangle\left\langle g_{4},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{4}\right\rangle\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\esssup_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{8}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{1},a_{2})\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{3},a_{4})\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{5},a_{6})\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{7},a_{8})\right)\left(\overline{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\varPhi}(0)^{2}\right\rVert_{\infty,\infty}^{2}\esssup_{\overline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\mathcal{E}}\left(\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{1},a_{2})\star\varphi^{(2)}(\cdot;a_{3},a_{4})\right)\left(\overline{x}\right)\leq C^{4}g(d).

(F.84)

Up to a constant, these are all less than or equal to the bounds we used for the
\left\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{7}\right\}=\left\{1,\ldots,1\right\}
case, and therefore that case dominates. These other cases would behave as constant multiple of one of
g(d)
,
g(d)^{\frac{3}{2}}
,
g(d)^{2}
,
g(d)^{\frac{5}{2}}
, and
g(d)^{3}
.

We have now dealt with all the cases which have no factors of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
. To demonstrate how we will deal with these, we first consider
\vec{j}=\left\{3,3,3,3,3,3,3,5\right\}
. For notational compactness, we define

\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}\right>_{I}:=\prod_{m\in I}\left\langle g_{m},\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}g_{m}\right\rangle,

(F.85)

where
I\subset\mathbb{N}
and
l\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since we have already extracted the factor of
\left(1-\mathbb{S}\left(\widehat{\varPhi}(k)\right)\right)
via the pointwise bound on
Q^{5}_{15,16}(l;k)
, we are left trying to bound the following integral:

\int\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2}-l_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l;k)\\
\qquad\times\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1,2,3,4}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{5,6,7,8}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{2})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{9,10}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{11,12}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{13,14}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}.

(F.86)

To bound this integral we need to partition our space. Fix
\varepsilon>0
and define

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle B_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{3}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle B_{4}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle B_{5}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\lvert l_{2}-l_{3}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}

\displaystyle B_{6}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\lvert l_{3}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l_{3}-k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l_{3}+k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\},

and for
n=0,1,\ldots,6
define

A_{n}:=\left\{(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)^{3}\colon\#\left\{m\colon(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})\in B_{m}\right\}=n\right\}.

(F.87)

In words,
A_{n}
is the set of points within
\varepsilon
(in Euclidean distance) of precisely
n
of the hyperplanes on which the
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
factors diverge. If we are working with a factor of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
on the set
B_{m}
associated with its relevant hyperplane, we use the bound
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\leq 1/\left(C_{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}\right)
, and if we are working off this set we use the bound
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\leq 1/\left(C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)
.

When we integrate over
A_{0}
, we bound
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\leq 1/\left(C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)
for all such factors and recycle the bound on the “only
\widehat{\varPhi}
” integrals from above. The contribution from
A_{0}
is therefore bounded by some constant multiple of

\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{18}}g(d)^{3}.

(F.88)

For
A_{1}
, first suppose we are considering
A_{1}\cap B_{m}
for
m\neq 6
. First we bound
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\leq 1/\left(C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l;k)\leq 3/\left(C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)
for the “irrelevant” directions, and use the pointwise bound for
\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}\right>_{m,n}
in the “relevant” direction. Then we can use the Cauchy-Schwarz and factorise argument we used above and recycle the integral bounds for the
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors as we did above. However we are careful about the way we split apart terms in the Cauchy-Schwarz step. We split the
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
terms across both factors and arrange the remaining
\widehat{\varPhi}
terms so that each factor has “directions” spanning
\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)^{3}
. To demonstrate we consider
A_{1}\cap B_{1}
:

\displaystyle\int_{A_{1}\cap B_{1}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{5,6,7,8}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{11,12}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{13,14}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq C^{2}\left(\int_{A_{1}\cap B_{1}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})^{2}\right>_{5,6}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})^{2}\right>_{11,12}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times\left(\int_{A_{1}\cap B_{1}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2})^{2}\right>_{7,8}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right>_{13,14}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.89)

After a change of variables, factorising and using our integral bounds of
\widehat{\varPhi}
terms from above, we are only left needing to bound

\int_{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\leq\frac{1}{C^{2}_{2}}\int_{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{4}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}=\frac{1}{C^{2}_{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-4}}{d-4}.

(F.90)

Therefore the contribution from
A_{1}\cap B_{m}
for
m\neq 6
is bounded by some constant multiple of
\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-18}}{d-4}g(d)^{2}
. For
A_{1}\cap B_{6}
we need to be slightly more careful because for
\left\lvert k\right\rvert<2\varepsilon
the constituent parts of
B_{6}
overlap - this is the same considerations we had to account for in the proof of Lemma
5.19
. We can overcome it in the same way by using Hölder’s inequality (actually just Cauchy-Schwarz in this case) and then re-centring the integrals. The net result is that the bound of a constant multiple of
\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-18}}{d-4}g(d)^{2}
also applies to
A_{1}\cap B_{6}
. In summary, the contribution from
A_{1}
is bounded by some constant multiple of

\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-18}}{d-4}g(d)^{2}.

(F.91)

The
A_{2}
case proceeds similarly. After partitioning the set according to which are the overlapping hyperplanes, we take the relevant
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
factors from these directions and one
\widehat{\varPhi}
factor from a linearly independent direction, and pointwise bound all the other
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
and
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors. The contribution from
A_{2}
is then bounded by some constant multiple of

\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2d-18}}{\left(d-4\right)^{2}}g(d),

(F.92)

arising from the
A_{1}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}
,
A_{1}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{6}
, and
A_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{6}
integrals.

The
A_{3}
case is more complicated because there are two qualitatively different sub-cases to consider. First suppose that the three normal vectors to the overlapping hyperplanes are linearly independent. Then we take the relevant
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
factors from these directions, pointwise bound the other
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
factors and all the
\widehat{\varPhi}
factors. The contribution from this sub-case of
A_{3}
is then bounded by some constant multiple of
\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{3}\frac{\varepsilon^{3d-18}}{\left(d-4\right)^{3}}
, arising from the
A_{1}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{6}
integral.

On the other hand, if the three normal vectors to the overlapping hyperplanes are linearly
dependent
, then we need to retain a
\widehat{\varPhi}
from the remaining linearly independent direction. To demonstrate, let us consider the
A_{3}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{4}
integral. After removing unnecessary terms via uniform bounds,

\displaystyle\int_{A_{3}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{4}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{13,14}\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})^{2}\right>^{\frac{1}{2}}_{11,12}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\int_{A_{3}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{4}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right>_{13,14}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\int_{A_{3}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{4}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{1}-l_{3})^{2}\right>_{11,12}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.93)

Applying a change of variables, factorising the integral, and applying Hölder’s inequality allows us to bound these parentheses:

\displaystyle\int_{A_{3}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\cap B_{4}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right>_{13,14}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2d}}\right)\left(\int\left<\widehat{\varPhi}(l)^{2}\right>_{13,14}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{C^{5}_{2}}\left(\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{1}\right\rvert^{4}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert^{4}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{2}\right\rvert^{2}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2d}}\right)g(d)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{C^{5}_{2}}\left(\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{1}\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert^{3}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2d}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{2}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{2}\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l_{1}-l_{2}\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}g(d)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{C^{5}_{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{d-6}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-6}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{d-3}\frac{\varepsilon^{d-3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}g(d).

(F.94)

For all of these linearly dependent cases, we can bound the integral by some constant multiple of
\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2d-18}}{\left(d-6\right)^{2}}g(d)
. In summary, the contribution from
A_{3}
is bounded by some constant multiple of

\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{3}\frac{\varepsilon^{3d-18}}{\left(d-4\right)^{3}}+\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2d-18}}{\left(d-6\right)^{2}}g(d).

(F.95)

For
A_{4}
,
A_{5}
, and
A_{6}
we don’t have the two linearly dependent/independent cases because any four of the directions spans
\left(\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)^{3}
. We therefore don’t need factors of
\widehat{\varPhi}
to control the unbounded directions. Instead, we need to be careful that when we split our integral at the Cauchy-Schwarz step the resulting parentheses have sufficiently few factors of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
that their bounds will be finite for
d>6
. The way we perform this split will be different for the different parts of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3};k)
. Let us define

\widehat{D}(l;k):=\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k).

(F.96)

We first consider
A_{6}
. By writing
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l;k)=\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{D}(l;k)+\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)
, we have two integrals we want to bound. First

\displaystyle\int_{A_{6}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2}-l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3})\widehat{D}(l_{3};k)\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\int_{A_{6}}\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{3}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})^{2}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3})\widehat{D}(l_{3};k)^{2}\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\int_{A_{6}}\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{3}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{3})^{2}\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.97)

We then perform the
l_{i}
integrals in specific orders. For the first factor we integrate over
l_{3}
(which factorises out already), then we integrate over
l_{2}
for fixed
l_{1}
, and finally integrate over
l_{1}
. For the second factor we integrate over
l_{1}
for fixed
l_{2}
and
l_{3}
, then we integrate over
l_{3}
for fixed
l_{2}
, and finally we integrate over
l_{2}
. There are therefore three forms of integral we need to bound:

\displaystyle\int_{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)^{3}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C^{3}_{2}}\int_{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}=\frac{1}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\frac{\varepsilon^{d-6}}{d-6}

(F.98)

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l-l^{\prime}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-l^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C^{3}_{2}}\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cap\left\{\left\lvert l-l^{\prime}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l-l^{\prime}\right\rvert^{4}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\int_{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}\left(\int_{\left\lvert l-l^{\prime}\right\rvert<\varepsilon}\frac{1}{\left\lvert l-l^{\prime}\right\rvert^{6}}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\frac{\varepsilon^{d-6}}{d-6}

(F.99)

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l+k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l)\widehat{D}(l;k)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{d-6}+\frac{1}{d-4}+\frac{1}{d-2}+\frac{1}{d}\right)\varepsilon^{d-6}.

(F.100)

In this last inequality,
C^{\prime}>0
is some uniform constant. The last inequality is derived by applying Hölder’s inequality in much the same way as for the second inequality, taking care to see when the three sets that are integrated over are overlapping. The calculation uses Hölder’s inequality and a partition of the space, and is similar to the calculation performed in the proof of Lemma
5.19
. The second integral we want to bound for
A_{6}
is

\displaystyle\int_{A_{6}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2}-l_{3})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}-k)\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\int_{A_{6}}\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}-k)^{2}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2}-l_{3})^{2}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})^{3}\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\int_{A_{6}}\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{2})^{3}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1})\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{1}-l_{2})^{2}\right]\left[\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}-l_{1})^{2}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l_{3}+k)\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}l_{1}\mathrm{d}l_{2}\mathrm{d}l_{3}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{3d}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(F.101)

We then perform the
l_{i}
integrals in specific orders. For the first factor we integrate over
l_{1}
(which factorises out already), then we integrate over
l_{2}
for fixed
l_{3}
, and finally integrate over
l_{3}
. For the second factor we integrate over
l_{3}
for fixed
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
, then we integrate over
l_{1}
for fixed
l_{2}
, and finally we integrate over
l_{2}
. The new integrals we need to bound are:

\int_{\left\{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l+k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-k)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\\
\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{d-6}+\frac{1}{d-4}+\frac{1}{d-2}+\frac{1}{d}\right)\varepsilon^{d-6}

(F.102)

\int_{\left(\left\{\left\lvert l\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l-k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\cup\left\{\left\lvert l+k\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}\right)\cap\left\{\left\lvert l-l^{\prime}\right\rvert<\varepsilon\right\}}\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l+k)\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}(l-l^{\prime})^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}l}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\\
\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{C^{3}_{2}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{d-6}+\frac{1}{d-4}\right)\varepsilon^{d-6}.

(F.103)

The calculation of these two bounds uses Hölder’s inequality and a partition of the space, and is similar to the calculation performed in the proof of Lemma
5.19
. The result of these bounds is that the integral in (
F.97
) is bounded by some constant multiple of

\frac{1}{\left(d-6\right)^{3}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{3}\varepsilon^{3d-18}.

(F.104)

For
A_{4}
and
A_{5}
we can repeat the argument for
A_{6}
, but note that in some places the bound
1/\left(C_{2}\left\lvert l\right\rvert^{2}\right)
will be replaced by
1/\left(C_{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)
. The net result of this is that the factor
\left(d-6\right)^{3}
can be replaced by
\left(d-6\right)^{\alpha_{0}}\left(d-4\right)^{\alpha_{1}}\left(d-2\right)^{\alpha_{2}}d^{\alpha_{3}}
for some
\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}\leq 0
such that
\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=3
. The net result is that the contribution from both
A_{4}
and
A_{5}
can also be bounded by some constant multiple of

\frac{1}{\left(d-6\right)^{3}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\right)^{3}\varepsilon^{3d-18}.

(F.105)

Above we have established bounds for the various parts of the integral when
\vec{j}=\left(3,3,3,3,3,3,3,5\right)
. Having
j_{8}=4
can also be dealt with by very similar arguments to that outlined above - the
l_{3}
integrals are in fact simpler. The above bounds still hold with the
\varepsilon^{-18}
becoming a
\varepsilon^{-16}
. Since
\varepsilon<1
, this produces a larger bound. Recall that up to a constant factor, having
j_{m}=1
produces a larger bound than
j_{m}=2
for
m=1,\ldots,7
. If we replace
j_{m}=3
with
j_{m}=1
, then after ignoring constant values we lose a factor of
\varepsilon^{-2}
, but we may also lose a factor of
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
unless that was for
m=1,2
(corresponding to
l_{1}
direction) or
m=3,4
(corresponding to
l_{2}
direction). Note that doing for both directions will lose a
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
factor.

If
\frac{1}{d}\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}\varepsilon^{d}=O\left(g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
for all fixed
\varepsilon>0
, then it is clear that the dominating bound is
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. This arises, for example from the bound for
\vec{j}=\left(1,1,1,1,1,1,1,1\right)
. The situation is more complicated if
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\frac{1}{d}\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}\varepsilon^{d}
for fixed
\varepsilon
, because we can choose to have
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(d)
and take it to
0
as
d\to\infty
. It is easy to see that all the
A_{1}
components (and
A_{2},\ldots,A_{6}
components) for each
\vec{j}
can be bounded by some constant multiple of
\frac{1}{d}\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}}{\left(2\pi\right)^{d}}\varepsilon^{d}
(provided
d>6
). We therefore only need to compare this to the contributions from
A_{0}
.

The bound we get from the above arguments for each
A_{0}
contribution are of the form
\varepsilon^{-2m}g(d)^{\frac{n}{2}}
, where
m
is the number of factors of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
and
n
is related to the multiplicity of
\varPhi
terms in each Fourier direction. To find the terms with the largest bound, we want to have as many factors of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
as possible without producing extra factors of
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Since
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll\frac{1}{d}\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}\varepsilon^{d}
for fixed
\varepsilon
, increasing
n
here instantly produces a smaller bound. By changing
j_{8}
from
1
to
4
or
5
, or any other element from
1
to
3
, we do gain a factor of
\widehat{G}_{\mu_{\lambda}}
but we also increase the number of
\varPhi
factors and therefore in most places we acquire extra factors of
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. The exception to this is for
j_{1}
and
j_{2}
, which correspond to the
l_{1}
directions, since they already produce a factor of
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. The
A_{0}
component of the
\vec{j}=\left(3,3,1,1,1,1,1,1\right)
term can be bounded by constant multiple of
\varepsilon^{-4}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We then optimise our choice of
\varepsilon
to have our two dominant bounds be of the same order. This produces
\varepsilon(d)^{2}=g(d)^{\frac{1}{d}}\frac{2\pi d}{\text{e}}\left(1+o(1)\right)
, and therefore our overall bound is given by

\varepsilon^{-4}g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}}=g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{d}}\left(\frac{\text{e}}{2\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(1+o(1)\right).

(F.106)

Since
g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{d}}\leq g(d)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{d}}
for sufficiently large
d
, this bound is of order
\beta^{2}
and our bound is proven.
∎
